Jo ties a rope from a branch of a tree overhanging a river. She stands on the bank holding the rope and then launches herself to swing out over the river and back to the bank where she stops.

The graph shows her distance from the bank at different times from the start.

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The pie chart has an angle of $90 \degree$ for the Tour de France.

How many people does this represent?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of people}     &= \dfrac {90} {360} \times 80 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {90 \times 80} {360} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {90 \times 4 \times 20} {90 \times 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4 \times 20} {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                            &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of people}     &= \dfrac {90} {360} \times 80 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {90 \times 80} {360} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {90 \times 4 \times 20} {90 \times 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4 \times 20} {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                            &= 20
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of the answers were for 'Euro $2016$'?

(Simplify your answer, if possible.)

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction}             &= \dfrac {16} {80} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {16} {16 \times 5} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {16}} {\cancel {16} \times 5} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1} {5}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {5}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {16}} {\cancel {16} \times 5} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1} {5}
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Angle for Euro 2016 sector}   &= \dfrac {16} {80} \times 360 \\\\

                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {16} \times \cancel 5 \times 72} {\cancel {16} \times \cancel 5} \\\\
                                    &= 72 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {16} \times \cancel 5 \times 72} {\cancel {16} \times \cancel 5} \\\\
                                    &= 72 \degree

There are ten pupils in the class.

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

![devonport-high-school-for-boys--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-7-00.png](/assets/devonport-high-school-for-boys--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-7-00.png "devonport-high-school-for-boys--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-7-00.png")

The chart shows the number of pupils choosing their favourite colours.

Which statement is wrong?

- Red received five more votes than yellow 
- There are thirty pupils in the class
- Four people like blue best
- More voted for green than orange
- One tenth like yellow best

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Difference between red and yellow $= 10 - 3 = 7$

The following statement is wrong:

Red received five more votes than yellow

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

Red received five more votes than yellow 

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Difference between red and yellow $= 10 - 3 = 7$
<emptyline>
The following statement is wrong:
<emptyline>
Red received five more votes than yellow
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Red received five more votes than yellow 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

![devonport-high-school-for-boys--11-plus--maths--2018--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-3-00.png](/assets/devonport-high-school-for-boys--11-plus--maths--2018--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-3-00.png "devonport-high-school-for-boys--11-plus--maths--2018--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-3-00.png")

What are the coordinates of the centre of the square?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{x-coordinates}    &= \dfrac {3 + 3 + 7 + 7} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
                        &= 5 \\\\
\text{y-coordinates}    &= \dfrac {0 + 4 + 4 + 0} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8} {4} \\\\
                        &= 2
\end {aligned}
$

Coordinates $= (5, 2)$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$(5, 2)$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text{x-coordinates}    &= \dfrac {3 + 3 + 7 + 7} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
                        &= 5 \\\\
\text{y-coordinates}    &= \dfrac {0 + 4 + 4 + 0} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8} {4} \\\\
                        &= 2
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Coordinates $= (5, 2)$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$(5, 2)$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p>devonport-high-school-for-boys--11-plus--maths--2018--sample-paper-3:1:38</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tgraph
</li>
<li>

</li>
<li>
tangle
</li>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

| Red | Blue | Green | Yellow | Orange |
|:---:|:----:|:-----:|:------:|:------:|
| $5$ |  $7$ |  $8$  |   $3$  |   $2$  |

A pie chart is drawn to show children’s favourite colour using the table above. What size angle would represent Red?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Angle red}                            &= \dfrac {5} {5 + 7 + 8 + 3 + 2} \times 360 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {5} {25} \times 360 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 360} {25} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 5 \times 72} {5 \times 5} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 5 \times 72} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
                                            &= 72 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$72 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Angle red}                            &= \dfrac {5} {5 + 7 + 8 + 3 + 2} \times 360 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {5} {25} \times 360 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 360} {25} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 5 \times 72} {5 \times 5} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 5 \times 72} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
                                            &= 72 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$72 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

$180$ children were surveyed as to their favourite pastime. The results are in the pie chart above. How many children prefer computers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children who prefer computers}                    &= \dfrac {186} {360} \times 180 \\\\
                                                        &= \dfrac {186 \times 180} {360} \\\\
                                                        &= \dfrac {93 \times 2 \times 180} {2 \times 180} \\\\
                                                        &= \dfrac {93 \times \cancel{2} \times \cancel{180}} {\cancel{2} \times \cancel{180}} \\\\
                                                        &= 93
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$93$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children who prefer computers}                    &= \dfrac {186} {360} \times 180 \\\\
                                                        &= \dfrac {186 \times 180} {360} \\\\
                                                        &= \dfrac {93 \times 2 \times 180} {2 \times 180} \\\\
                                                        &= \dfrac {93 \times \cancel{2} \times \cancel{180}} {\cancel{2} \times \cancel{180}} \\\\
                                                        &= 93
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_g2 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>A9449EF9-3053-4405-96FD-35534110FAFD</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>devonport-high-school-for-boys--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-5:1:14</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tgraph
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

![devonport-high-school-for-boys--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-5/section-1-question-14-00.png](/assets/devonport-high-school-for-boys--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-5/section-1-question-14-00.png "devonport-high-school-for-boys--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-5/section-1-question-14-00.png")

$40$ students were asked which was their favourite colour. How many students chose Yellow?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Students who choose yellow}               &= \dfrac {45} {360} \times 40 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {45 \times 40} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {45 \times 8 \times 5} {45 \times 8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {45} \times \cancel 8 \times 5} {\cancel {45} \times \cancel 8} \\\\
                                                &= 5
\end{aligned}

                                                &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down the fraction of the school children who liked tennis, giving your answer in its lowest form.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children who liked tennis}                &= \dfrac {45} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {45} {45 \times 8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {45}} {\cancel {45}  \times 8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {1} {8}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {8}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children who liked tennis}                &= \dfrac {45} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {45} {45 \times 8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {45}} {\cancel {45}  \times 8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {1} {8}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {8}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A total of $240$ children were asked to complete the survey. Calculate how many of the children preferred cricket.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children who liked cricket}               &= \dfrac {60} {360} \times 240 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {60 \times 240} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {60 \times 6 \times 40} {60 \times 6} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel{60} \times \cancel{6} \times 40} {\cancel{60} \times \cancel{6}} \\\\
                                                &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children who liked cricket}               &= \dfrac {60} {360} \times 240 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {60 \times 240} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {60 \times 6 \times 40} {60 \times 6} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel{60} \times \cancel{6} \times 40} {\cancel{60} \times \cancel{6}} \\\\
                                                &= 40
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$40$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Estimate how many children would say their favorite sport was football out of the whole school of $1200$ pupils.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children who liked football}              &= \dfrac {360 - 90 - 45 - 60 - 30} {360} \times 1200 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {360 - 225} {360} \times 1200 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {135} {360} \times 1200 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {135 \times 1200} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3 \times 45 \times 120 \times 10} {120 \times 3} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 45 \times \cancel {120} \times 10} {\cancel {120} \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                                &= 45 \times 10 \\\\
                                                &= 450
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$450$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                                                &= \dfrac {360 - 225} {360} \times 1200 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {135} {360} \times 1200 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {135 \times 1200} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3 \times 45 \times 120 \times 10} {120 \times 3} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 45 \times \cancel {120} \times 10} {\cancel {120} \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                                &= 45 \times 10 \\\\
                                                &= 450
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A packet containing seeds says it produces white, pink and blue flowers in such a proportion that, on average, 
for every one white flower grown there will be two pink and three blue flowers.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

I plant some seeds and get three white flowers. Write down how many blue flowers I should expect to grow.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the expected number of blue flowers $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{white} : 3 \ \text{blue}              &= 3 \ \text{white} : a \ \text{blue} \\\\
\dfrac{1} {3}                                   &= \dfrac {3} {a} \\\\
1 \times a                                      &= 3 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

Expected number of blue flowers $= 9$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{white}                  &:& \text{blue} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& 3 \\\\
= & 1 \times 3                    &:& 3 \times 3 \\\\
= & 3                             &:& 9
\end{array}
$

Expected number of blue flowers $= 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the expected number of blue flowers $= a$

1 \times a                                      &= 3 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Expected number of blue flowers $= 9$
</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$

= & 3                             &:& 9
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Expected number of blue flowers $= 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

I plant a total of $30$ seeds in another patch of the garden. Calculate how many white flowers I would expect to grow in this patch.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the expected number of white flowers $= a$

Expected number of pink flowers $= 2a$

Expected number of blue flowers $= 3a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2a + 3a         &= 30 \\\\
6a                  &= 30 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {30} {6} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {6 \times 5} {6} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 5} {\cancel 6} \\\\
a                   &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

Expected number of white flowers $= 5$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{seeds}                                      &:& \text{white} \\\\
= & 1 + 2 + 3                                         &:& 1 \\\\
= & 6                                                 &:& 1 \\\\
= & 6 \times 5                                        &:& 1 \times 5 \\\\
= & 30                                                &:& 5
\end{array}
$

Expected number of white flowers $= 5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>

\end{aligned}

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

In another area of the garden, $24$ blue flowers are grown. Calculate how many pink flowers I should expect to find here.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the expected number of pink flowers $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{blue} : 2 \ \text{pink}               &= 24 \ \text{blue} : a \ \text{pink} \\\\
\dfrac{3}{2}                                    &= \dfrac {24} {a} \\\\
3 \times a                                      &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {24 \times 2} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 3 \times 2} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel 3 \times 2} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 8 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= 16
\end{aligned}
$

Expected number of pink flowers $= 16$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{blue}                               &:& \text{pink} \\\\
= & 3                                         &:& 2 \\\\
= & 3 \times 8                                &:& 2 \times 8 \\\\
= & 24                                        &:& 16
\end{array}
$

Expected number of pink flowers $= 16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

3 \times a                                      &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {24 \times 2} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 3 \times 2} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel 3 \times 2} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 8 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= 16
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Expected number of pink flowers $= 16$
</code></pre>
</div>

= & 24                                        &:& 16
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If I pick a flower at random from the $150$ grown in the test, list the following possible outcomes in order of likelihood,

-  picking a red or orange flower
-  picking a purple flower
-  picking an orange or yellow flower
-  picking a yellow flower

</div>

\text{orange or yellow flower} &= \dfrac {58 + 72} {150} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {130} {150} \\\\
\text{yellow flower}           &= \dfrac {72} {150}
\end{aligned}
$

-  picking a yellow flower

-  picking an orange or yellow flower

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

<div class='workings'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down the fraction of the children who said Rugby was their favourite, giving your answer in its lowest form.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of children who liked rugby}     &= \dfrac {45} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {45} {45 \times 8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {45}} {\cancel {45}  \times 8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {1} {8}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {8}$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {45}} {\cancel {45}  \times 8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {1} {8}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A total of $240$ children were asked to complete the survey. Calculate how many of the children listed Football as their favourite.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children who liked football}              &= \dfrac {180 - 45} {360} \times 240 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {135} {360} \times 240 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {135 \times 240} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3 \times 45 \times 120 \times 2} {120 \times 3} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 45 \times \cancel {120} \times 2} {\cancel {120} \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                                &= 45 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 90
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$90$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_on_a_straight_line
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children who liked football}              &= \dfrac {180 - 45} {360} \times 240 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {135} {360} \times 240 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {135 \times 240} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3 \times 45 \times 120 \times 2} {120 \times 3} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 45 \times \cancel {120} \times 2} {\cancel {120} \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                                &= 45 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 90
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The whole school has $1320$ pupils. Estimate how many of them you would expect to describe Swimming as their favourite sport, judging by the information in the pie chart. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children who liked swimmimg}              &= \dfrac {60} {360} \times 1320 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {60 \times 1320} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {60 \times 220 \times 6} {60 \times 6} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {60} \times 220 \times \cancel 6} {\cancel {60} \times \cancel 6} \\\\
                                                &= 220
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$220$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children who liked swimmimg}              &= \dfrac {60} {360} \times 1320 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {60 \times 1320} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {60 \times 220 \times 6} {60 \times 6} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {60} \times 220 \times \cancel 6} {\cancel {60} \times \cancel 6} \\\\
                                                &= 220
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$220$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

No-one in the group had more than three siblings, and the results are shown in the pie chart below.

![dulwich-college--11-plus--maths--9999--specimen-paper-e/section-1-question-8-00.png](/assets/dulwich-college--11-plus--maths--9999--specimen-paper-e/section-1-question-8-00.png "dulwich-college--11-plus--maths--9999--specimen-paper-e/section-1-question-8-00.png")

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down the percentage of children who have two siblings.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{\% of children with 2 siblings}           &= \dfrac {90} {360} \times 100 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {90 \times 100} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {90 \times 4 \times 25} {90 \times 4} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4 \times 25} {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                                &= 25\%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$25 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{\% of children with 2 siblings}           &= \dfrac {90} {360} \times 100 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {90 \times 100} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {90 \times 4 \times 25} {90 \times 4} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4 \times 25} {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                                &= 25\%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$25 \%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the fraction of children who have no siblings, giving your answer in its lowest terms.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Angles around a point add up to} = 360 \degree$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction}                                 &= \dfrac {360 - 45 - 90 - 90} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {135} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {45 \times 3} {45 \times 8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {45} \times 3} {\cancel {45} \times 8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3} {8}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {3} {8}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_around_a_point
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction}                                 &= \dfrac {360 - 45 - 90 - 90} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {135} {360} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {45 \times 3} {45 \times 8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {45} \times 3} {\cancel {45} \times 8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3} {8}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {3} {8}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$8$ of the children who were surveyed had one sibling.

Fill in the table below to show the number of children who have $0, 2$ and $3$ siblings.

|      Number of siblings     | $0$ | $1$ | $2$ | $3$ |
|:---------------------------:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| Number of children surveyed |     | $8$ |     |     |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of children surveyed $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {45} {360} \times a          &= 8 \\\\
a                                   &= 8 \times \dfrac {360} {45} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {8 \times 360} {45} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {8 \times 8 \times 45} {45} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {8 \times 8 \times \cancel {45}} {\cancel {45}} \\\\
a                                   &= 8 \times 8 \\\\
a                                   &= 64
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children with no siblings}    &= \dfrac {135} {360} \times 64 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {135 \times 64} {360} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {45 \times 3 \times 8 \times 8} {45 \times 8} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {45} \times 3 \times 8 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel {45} \times \cancel 8} \\\\
                                    &= 24 \\\\
\text{Children with 2 siblings}     &= \dfrac {90} {360} \times 64 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {90 \times 64} {360} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {90 \times 4 \times 16} {90 \times 4} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4 \times 16} {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                    &= 16 \\\\
\text{Children with 3 siblings}     &= \dfrac {90} {360} \times 64 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {90 \times 64} {360} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {90 \times 4 \times 16} {90 \times 4} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4 \times 16} {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                    &= 16
\end{aligned}
$

|      Number of siblings     | $0$ | $1$ | $2$ | $3$ |
|:---------------------------:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| Number of children surveyed | $24$| $8$ | $16$| $16$|

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of children surveyed $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

a                                   &= \dfrac {8 \times 8 \times 45} {45} \\\\

a                                   &= 64
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

                                    &= \dfrac {45 \times 3 \times 8 \times 8} {45 \times 8} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {45} \times 3 \times 8 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel {45} \times \cancel 8} \\\\
                                    &= 24 \\\\
\text{Children with 2 siblings}     &= \dfrac {90} {360} \times 64 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {90 \times 64} {360} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {90 \times 4 \times 16} {90 \times 4} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4 \times 16} {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                    &= 16 \\\\
\text{Children with 3 siblings}     &= \dfrac {90} {360} \times 64 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {90 \times 64} {360} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {90 \times 4 \times 16} {90 \times 4} \\\\

                                    &= 16
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate the area of $ABCD$. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Height of triangle ABC}   &= 5 - 3 \\\\
                                &= 2 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Base of triangle ABC}     &= 6 - 0 \\\\
                                &= 6 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Area of triangle ABC}     &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \text{Height of triangle ABC} \times \text{Base of triangle ABC} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 2 \times 6 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 6} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                &= 6 \ {\text{cm}} ^ 2 \\\\
\text{Area of kite ABCD}        &= 2 \times \text{Area of triangle ABC} \\\\
                                &= 2 \times 6 \\\\
                                &= 12 \ {\text{cm}} ^ 2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12 \ {\text{cm}} ^ 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Height of triangle ABC}   &= 5 - 3 \\\\
                                &= 2 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Base of triangle ABC}     &= 6 - 0 \\\\
                                &= 6 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Area of triangle ABC}     &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \text{Height of triangle ABC} \times \text{Base of triangle ABC} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 2 \times 6 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 6} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                &= 6 \ {\text{cm}} ^ 2 \\\\
\text{Area of kite ABCD}        &= 2 \times \text{Area of triangle ABC} \\\\
                                &= 2 \times 6 \\\\
                                &= 12 \ {\text{cm}} ^ 2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12 \ {\text{cm}} ^ 2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Difference}     &= 18 - 14 \\\\
                      &= 4 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workings'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average time}     &= \dfrac {80} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 16} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 16} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                        &= 16 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

Peter walked at $4\ \text{km/h}$. 

How far is it around the school field? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Distance} = \text{Speed} \times \text{Time}$


ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance}     &= 4 \ \text{km/h} \times \dfrac {15} {60} \ \text{h} \\\\
                    &= 4 \ \text{km/h} \times \dfrac {15} {60} \ \text{h} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 15} {60} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 15} {4 \times 15} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times \cancel {15}} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel {15}} \\\\
                    &= 1 \ \text{km}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1 \ \text{km}$

</div>
</div>

                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 15} {60} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 15} {4 \times 15} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times \cancel {15}} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel {15}} \\\\
                    &= 1 \ \text{km}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A sheet of graph paper is placed with its x-axis pointing due East and its y-axis pointing due North. 
A sluggish snail starts at point $(0, 0)$ and slowly, 
but smoothly, slithers $1$ unit North, $2$ units East, $3$ units South, $4$ units West, 
$5$ units North, $6$ units East, $7$ units South, $8$ units West, $9$ units North and (lastly!) $10$ units East. 

At which point does the snail finally arrive? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)



Draw the path of the snail and also write the length of each path

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{x-coordinates}    &= 2 E + 4 W + 6 E + 8 W + 10 E \\\\
                        &= 2 - 4 + 6 - 8 + 10 \\\\
                        &= 6 \\\\
\text{y-coordinates}    &= 1 N + 3 S + 5 N + 7 S + 9 N \\\\
                        &= 1 - 3 + 5 - 7 + 9 \\\\
                        &= 5
\end{aligned}
$


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


Point at which finally snail arrive $= (6, 5)$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$(6, 5)$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                        &= 6 \\\\
\text{y-coordinates}    &= 1 N + 3 S + 5 N + 7 S + 9 N \\\\
                        &= 1 - 3 + 5 - 7 + 9 \\\\
                        &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_add_drawing_ends
<emptyline>
Point at which finally snail arrive $= (6, 5)$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

In which country did $240$ people own a computer?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Computers in pictograph}              &= \dfrac {240} {50} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {240 \times 2} {50 \times 2} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {480} {100} \\\\
                                            &= 4.8
\end {aligned}
$

Country = Korea

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Korea

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Computers in pictograph}              &= \dfrac {240} {50} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {240 \times 2} {50 \times 2} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {480} {100} \\\\
                                            &= 4.8
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Country = Korea
</code></pre>
</div>
